| 2026-08-01 | **External module install: implemented and verified on real installations — `EXTERNAL_MODULE_INSTALL_DESIGN.md` is no longer a proposal, and the capability audit it deferred is closed.** All six steps of its §6, on `fix-build-packages` (13 further commits, `2cafb0b1fe..`). **The privilege split:** `sudo modcompile rebuild`/`add-gomod` now runs root → drop → root — root records the module source and regenerates `go.mod`/`imports_generated.go`, a re-exec as an unprivileged `stratumak-build` account resolves dependencies and compiles, root installs the staged binary and reapplies capabilities. Codegen stays privileged deliberately (it runs no module-supplied code); `go build`, cgo and gcc never do. `modcompile --install` got the same treatment rather than being left as the one place a compiler still ran as root: root stages the generated C plus the source directory's headers where the build identity can read them — which also means the `.comp` may sit in a `0700` home directory, and usually does. **Layout:** `/var/lib/stratumak/modules` holds the root-owned copy of each registered module and is the source of truth; `/var/lib/stratumak/stmak` is derived from the pristine sources plus those copies and regenerated in full on every rebuild, which is what makes an upgrade of the sources reach the next build; `/usr/bin/stmakd` → `/var/lib/stratumak/bin/stmakd` → `/usr/libexec/stratumak/stmakd`, the last never written, so an upgrade cannot discard a local build and never has to recover from having done so. Nothing under `/var` is shipped — postinst creates it, `postrm purge` removes it, and `debian/rules` deletes it from the staging tree, because if dpkg owned that symlink every upgrade would unpack it over a locally rebuilt server in silence. **Four rulings (maintainer, 2026-08-01):** a cmod name present in both directories is **refused, not shadowed** (this settles the shadow-vs-collide question the design left open); the build identity is **always `stratumak-build`**, `SUDO_UID` only where the account does not exist; upgrades **warn, never rebuild** (no systemd one-shot); the setcap list is carried across **verbatim**. **postinst now applies the realtime capabilities**, closing the previous entry's "nothing grants realtime privileges on a packaged system" — dpkg does not preserve xattrs, so they must be re-applied on every install and upgrade. **The capability audit is closed and nothing on the list can be removed**, which answers `make setuid`'s long-standing `TODO: check what's actually needed`. A source audit called `cap_perfmon` and `cap_sys_admin` unused; three runs on a real EtherCAT machine (6.12 RT, `xdp-native`) said otherwise, each failing differently: dropping `cap_perfmon` alone works **only because `cap_sys_admin` also satisfies `perfmon_capable()`**; dropping both gives `R1 pointer comparison prohibited` from the BPF verifier; dropping `cap_sys_admin` alone gives `couldn't get program fd: Operation not permitted` from the ID-based BPF lookups libxdp uses, which `CAP_BPF` deliberately does not grant. Neither requirement is in this tree — both are demanded by the kernel on behalf of a program shipped inside libxdp, so no grep could have found them, and removing capabilities one at a time hides the answer when two satisfy the same gate. `cap_dac_override` was understated the same way: it is not a Pi/GPIO nicety but the mainstream path, needed to write `/proc/irq/$n/smp_affinity` (IRQ pinning), `/dev/cpu_dma_latency` (PM QoS floor) and `/sys/bus/pci/devices/*/resource0`, all root-owned files opened by a process that deliberately is not root; removing it would not have failed cleanly, it would have reverted latency to whatever the housekeeping CPU is doing. **The blocker behind all of it: the installed stratuMAK tree had never been buildable**, so a rebuild on a packaged system was impossible whatever the permissions said — the design's opening `permission denied` was the first obstacle, not the only one. Five independent causes, all fixed: `stmak-install` shipped only `*.go`, leaving every cgo package without its C sources (26 now, was 9); eleven headers were installed nowhere, one of them listed under a path it had left years ago and swallowed by a `-cp` that ignores errors; several packages include headers the way the source tree does (`"hal/hal_priv.h"`), which a flat include directory cannot answer, so headers now install a **second** time under their source-relative path; four C files are `#include`d textually (`cnc/tp/tc.c` and friends) and install alongside them; and `cgoFlags` put its include path in `CGO_CFLAGS`, which never reaches the C++ compiler, so `interp_shim.cc` could not find `config.h` while the C beside it compiled. **Two bugs only a real machine found, both of which the code had convinced me were fine:** `getcap` `lstat()`s its argument and silently skips anything that is not a regular file, so reading capabilities off `$(bindir)/stmakd` — a symlink until the first local rebuild — returned the same answer as "this file has none", and the rebuilt server was installed unable to do realtime, saying only that there had been nothing to carry over (the design note asserted the opposite, and the code repeated it in a comment); and `compileToSO` passed `-I$(EMC2Home)/include`, correct only for a run-in-place tree, so on a packaged system **no component including `rtapi_math.h` could compile at all** — pre-existing, `--compile` reproduces it with no root involved, and it survived because the components installed until then happened not to need the maths headers. **Build-system repairs that fell out:** almost no cmod rule depended on the stratuMAK headers, survivable only until the new cmod ABI stamp made a skipped rebuild a load-time refusal (one line in `src/Makefile` now covers all 218); `STMAK_SRC_BASE` did not glob `*.cc` although its comment claimed the interp shim was covered, so the one C++ translation unit in the tree was exactly the file an edit to which rebuilt nothing; the tree mirror judged files up to date on size and mtime, which misses a same-length edit an upgrade can deliver, and now compares content; and `cnc/rs274ngc/interp_shim.h` and `stmak/internal/task/interp_shim.h` shared a basename **and** an include guard, with the flat copy silently beating the second inside its own package — the stratuMAK one is renamed `task_interp_shim.h` and `make check-header-collisions` now fails the build on the next such clash. **Verified on real installations, not by inspection:** `src/stmak/cmd/modcompile/verify-privileged-rebuild.sh` passes all six sections (its first run is what caught the `getcap` bug); an out-of-tree Go module goes through `make install` → `sudo modcompile add-gomod .` with the project compiling as the invoking user and only the `modcompile` call escalating; a real component set installs to `/var/lib/stratumak/cmod` and loads from there by bare name at startup and at runtime; and putting a copy of one into the package's own directory makes the launcher refuse it naming both full paths — the one behaviour where a bug would mean the wrong module loading in silence rather than anything visible failing. **Open, unchanged from the previous entry:** full runtests still has not run on this branch (the tree is `RUN_IN_PLACE = no`; it needs a RIP rebuild first) and remains the only genuine regression gate owed; no CI job checks package contents or installs the deb and starts a config; the pre-existing lintian tail; dead `EMC2_GOMOD_DIR` in `configure`/`config.h.in`; and `docs/po/*.po`. **Newly open:** `man modcompile` documents the *Mesa Modbus* compiler (now `mesa-modcompile`) rather than the stratuMAK one, and `docs/src/drivers/mesa_modbus.adoc` still calls that tool `modcompile`; no user-facing document describes the external-module workflow at all. |
| 2026-08-01 | **`fix-build-packages`: the packaging branch — 40 commits (`1c5892b361..7dae241895`, i.e. everything past the `fix-axis-tools` deliverable), build warnings 884 → 2, and four install-layout defects that only a real installation could find.** The two survivors are `dpkg-shlibdeps: diversions involved`, an artifact of the build host's in-progress usr-merge; they will not appear in a CI chroot. Everything else was a real defect that happened not to fail the build: `_FORTIFY_SOURCE` was effectively 2 while the tree documented intent as 3 (171 redefinition warnings); asciidoc's backends define `[sect1]`–`[sect4]` only, so every `sect5` heading was silently dropped from output (660); a dangling `<1>`/`<2>` callout pair in `qtvcp.adoc` — prose explains them inline, no callout list — leaked through asciidoc's *global* callout map, which is only reset when a list closes, and made the **next** included file warn instead; the German masters translated the attribute *name* (`{date}` → `{datum}`/`{Datum}`), and asciidoc drops any line containing an undefined attribute, so `Master_Getting_Started` and `Master_Integrator` built with an empty title (fixed in `de.po` too, or po4a puts it back); asciidoc looks for `lang-<lang>.conf` only in its own conf dir, `/usr/share/asciidoc` and `$HOME/.asciidoc` — never a `--conf-file` path — so `nb` and `zh_CN` silently fell back to English captions (both confs added, staged into a private `HOME` for asciidoc/a2x; `nb` captions taken from docbook-xsl's `nb.xml`); and the `toggle`/`toggle2nist` timing diagrams used box-drawing characters the PostScript device has no glyphs for, so those diagrams were simply absent from the PDF manual. `debian/shlibs.local` was dropped after verifying `shlibs:Depends` comes out byte-identical without it — it was a copy of the package's own `DEBIAN/shlibs` plus two entries for `libncurses5` and `libpth`, and its only remaining effect was a warning per unsplittable SONAME. **Package border settled by ruling** (creating a cmod and adding a gomod are integrator operations, not operator ones): `modcompile`, `mesa-modcompile` and the 408-file Go tree to `-dev`, with `build-essential` and `golang-go` as hard `-dev` deps; `mesambccc` stays in base because it emits a control-command *data* file and needs no compiler. Upstream 2.9.8 is the oracle — `git show 39bfc1874a:debian/linuxcnc-uspace-dev.install` puts both compilers in `-dev` and `mesambccc` in base (note its `/usr/bin/modcompile` is the Mesa Modbus one, our `mesa-modcompile`). **Changelog restarted**: `stratumak (0.1.0)` alone, LinuxCNC history moved to `debian/changelog.linuxcnc` minus the two modusoft revisions, which also silenced `dpkg-genchanges: current version is older than the previous`. **The dominant defect class, and why building was never going to be enough: a path fixed at build time naming something the package never installs.** Four instances, all silent — a missing `-I` is not an error to gcc, and a missing module directory just falls through to another lookup: `install-modules` put all ~200 cmods in `EMC2_GOMOD_DIR` while `stmakd` has `EMC2_CMOD_DIR` baked in, so **no C module could load on an installed system at all** (every `load` fell through to the compiled-in Go registry and died on the first name, `no Go module "tpmod" in registry`) and `debian/*.install` claims `usr/lib/stratumak` wholesale so the deb packaged whatever directory the install invented; `ETHERCAT_LIBS` passed `-Wl,-rpath,$(ECMASTER_PREFIX)/lib` unconditionally, shipping the *build tree's* absolute path as `ethercat.so`'s RUNPATH; `EMC2_CMOD_INCLUDE_DIR` was `$(includedir)/stratumak/cmod`, which nothing installs, so `modcompile <x>.comp --install` died on its first `#include`; and `pkg/hal`'s `${SRCDIR}`-relative `#cgo CFLAGS` resolve only inside the source tree, so an out-of-tree Go module importing it failed on `#include "hal.h"` — `modcompile` had computed the right `CGO_CFLAGS` for its own server rebuilds all along and kept it to itself, now shared as `cgoFlags()` and emitted by `--print-make-inc` as `STMAK_CGO_CFLAGS`/`STMAK_CGO_LDFLAGS`. `EMC2_GOMOD_DIR` turns out to have **no readers anywhere** — Go modules are compiled into `stmakd` and register at `init()`, never loaded from a directory — and two comments in `halparse/token.go` that claimed otherwise were corrected. **Two co-installability bugs found by diffing the two `.deb`s**, not by reading a log: `/usr/bin/modcompile` and 118 `usr/share/man/man3/*` pages were each claimed by *both* packages with no `Replaces` between them, so `apt install stratumak-dev` would have aborted on every one. **The verification that works:** extract the deb, ask the *packaged* binary what paths it believes (`modcompile --include-dir`, `--print-make-inc`, `strings` for baked dirs), assert each answer exists in some package, then compile a real `.comp` and a real Go module using nothing but those values. **Open:** full runtests has never run on this branch and the tree is `RUN_IN_PLACE = no`, so it needs a RIP rebuild first — it is the only genuine regression gate still owed on these 40 commits; `EXTERNAL_MODULE_INSTALL_DESIGN.md` is proposal-only (privilege split, `/var/lib/stratumak`, symlinked active server, staleness instead of postinst rebuilds) with the local-cmod shadow-vs-collide question undecided; `docs/po/*.po` are rewritten by every doc build and have not been committed since 2022, and committing the regeneration marks many entries `fuzzy`, i.e. renders them untranslated; the lintian tail (203 `debug-file-with-no-debug-symbols` and 5 `statically-linked-binary`, both Go artefacts wanting overrides, plus 8 `no-manual-page`, 8 `icon-size-and-directory-name-mismatch`, 7 `groff-message`, 3 `bad-whatis-entry`, and `custom-library-search-path` for `stmakd`'s redundant `RPATH /usr/lib`); dead `EMC2_GOMOD_DIR` still present in `configure`/`config.h.in`; nothing grants realtime capabilities on a packaged system (`make setuid` is RIP-only, the postinst sets only `memlock`); and **no CI job checks package contents or installs the deb and starts a config** — the two jobs that would have caught every install-layout defect above, since `runtests` exercises the RIP tree where the layout differs. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Package renamed to `stratumak` 0.1.0; the vestigial `install-kernel-*` targets renamed; three packaging defects fixed.** `linuxcnc-uspace` → **`stratumak`**, `-dev` and the five `-doc-<lang>` packages with it, source package too. The `-uspace` suffix went with the rename: it distinguished the userspace flavour from an RTAI one that `debian/configure` disables with `if false`, so there was nothing left to distinguish from. **Rulings:** drop the suffix; `Conflicts` + `Replaces` + `Provides` against `linuxcnc`/`linuxcnc-uspace` (they own the same paths, so the two cannot co-install; Replaces takes an existing install over in one step, Provides keeps dependants satisfied); rename the doc packages too, so they stop colliding with upstream's; `0.1.0` in **both** `VERSION` (hence `EMC2VERSION` and what UIs report) and the changelog, **epoch dropped** — a renamed package has no upgrade ordering to preserve. Internal paths deliberately untouched: `usr/share/stratumak`, `/usr/bin/linuxcnc` and the rest stay. **Four things a plain search-and-replace would have missed:** `debian/update-dch-from-git` hard-codes the source name and regenerates the top changelog entry in CI, so it would have renamed the source package straight back; lintian overrides and `doc-base` `Document:` fields are keyed by package name and would have stopped applying silently; `rules.in` hard-codes the doc packages' *staging directories* (and `--build=any` skips the indep half, so an arch-only build would have passed while a full build broke); and `stratumak.install.in` still named `debian/linuxcnc-uspace.metainfo.xml` — a source-relative `source destination` entry, the one shape the earlier install-list validation skipped, now checked too. Fixed an inconsistency in passing: the German doc stanza used a literal `linuxcnc-uspace` where its four siblings used the macro. **`install-kernel-dep`/`-indep` → `install-modules`/`install-common`** (user observation): the split was kernel-dependent vs -independent when the realtime layer was RTAI kernel modules; nothing here is kernel-related now, and nothing outside `src/Makefile` referenced the old names. **`dh_shlibdeps -l`** was given a relative path, which reaches `dpkg-shlibdeps` as an absolute `/debian/…` and silently matches nothing — the package's own libraries were never on the search path it existed to add. **`man3` pages** were staged by the install and listed in no `.manpages` file, so they were built and dropped. **The postinst now runs at all.** `debian/linuxcnc.postinst` was keyed to a package name that has never existed (the package was `linuxcnc-uspace`), so it had never executed — meaning the memlock limit realtime needs was never set and the pendant udev rules the package ships were never reloaded. Renamed to `stratumak.postinst`, with `invoke-rc.d udev restart` replaced by a guarded `udevadm control --reload-rules`: there is no running udev in a chroot or container and `policy-rc.d` makes `invoke-rc.d` exit 101, which under `set -e` would have aborted the installation — including CI's own `apt-get install ../*.deb`. The `#EMC2` marker in `limits.conf` is kept deliberately, so a machine that has ever had LinuxCNC installed does not get a second line. Dropped `debian/linuxcnc.sharedmimeinfo`, a byte-identical dead duplicate keyed to the same nonexistent package. **Verified on the artifact:** `stratumak_0.1.0_amd64.deb` + `-dbgsym` + `-dev` + `-dev-dbgsym`, built with `dpkg-buildpackage --build=any DEB_BUILD_OPTIONS=parallel=4`, `dh_missing` silent. Control reports `Package: stratumak`, `Version: 0.1.0`, and the three rename relationships; the shipped postinst carries debhelper's `py3compile -p stratumak:amd64` (so debhelper sees the new name) plus the guarded reload and the memlock line, with no `invoke-rc.d`. Contents intact across the rename: stmakd, modcompile, halcmd, ethercat, manualtoolchange_ui, 208 cmods, 7 webapps, 408 stratuMAK tree files, 3 udev rules, 2 metainfo files, and the full earlier check suite green. RIP tree rebuilt clean. **A `postrm` now removes the memlock line on purge** (`66fefd887b`) — the postinst had appended it since forever with nothing to take it back. Purge only (on remove, configuration stays, and a realtime limit is configuration), matched on the exact shape the postinst writes so a hand-written memlock line, a commented-out copy or an unrelated EMC2 comment survives, and never fatal — a failing postrm leaves a package half-purged and needing manual dpkg surgery. Consequence worth knowing: the postinst deliberately matches upstream LinuxCNC's `#EMC2` marker so it never appends a duplicate, which means purging stratumak also clears a line an earlier LinuxCNC install left. Intended — the two cannot co-install and stratumak `Replaces` it. Verified by round-tripping the **shipped** scripts against each other: the line `stratumak.postinst` writes is matched and removed by `stratumak.postrm`'s pattern, while `@realtime - memlock unlimited` survives; non-purge invocations (`remove`, `upgrade`, `abort-install`, `disappear`) exit 0 and leave the file byte-identical. Open: `debian/tests/linuxcnc-test*` script filenames still carry the old name (internal, deferred with the other internal renames). |

This is the frozen-as-live class again, this time built into the code generator. Restricted to **struct** returns by construction: a delta of a `map[string]T` is still a well-formed map and classicladder/pyvcp merge it into their own keyed stores by design, so those clients are byte-identical. ② The TS REST generator emitted a reviver for every 64-bit-carrying type in the API, including types only reachable through *watch* functions it does not serve — dead code that fails `noUnusedLocals`; revivers are now emitted only where called. | 2026-07-31 | **CRITICAL: an MDI feed move executed nothing and reported success — the naive-CAM chain was never closed at the end of an MDI line, and the next line dropped it.** Found while smoke-testing the ported `mdi` CLI against `configs/sim/axis/axis_mm.ini`: `G1 X5 Y5` from MDI moved the machine **not at all**, returned RCS_DONE, logged no error, and left `queue`/`motion_id`/`motion_type`/`distance_to_go` at 0 — nothing ever reached motion. `G0` to the same point worked, and the identical G-code run as an AUTO program worked, which is exactly why it hid. **Mechanism** (each step verified against the 2.9 source): 2.9's `see_segment` buffers *every* `STRAIGHT_FEED` into the naive-CAM chain and flushes only when the next point cannot link onto it — so a feed move ends an MDI execution with the chain still OPEN. Nothing closes it: `Interp::_execute` calls `canon.finish()` only on its o-word branch (`rs274ngc_pre.cc`), which a plain block never reaches. 2.9 survives that because the chain simply waits for the next command's first canon call (with the default `naivecamTolerance == 0`, `linkable()` is false, so the very next feed/traverse/dwell flushes it). stratuMAK does not: `executeMDI` calls `canon.syncEndPointFromMachine()` on entry, which **drops** the chain (faithful to 2.9 `GET_EXTERNAL_POSITION` → `drop_segments`, but 2.9 never runs it before an MDI line) — so the buffered move was destroyed, not deferred. The pre-MDI resync is original (`fd9eb94104`); it only became destructive when the naive-CAM detector landed (2026-07-15, the G64-blending work) and feed moves started being buffered at all. **Fix:** `canon.flushSegments()` after a successful interp execution, in **both** MDI paths — `executeMDI` (plain block), and `finishMDI`'s continuation loop (the tail of an o-word sub resumed after a queue buster; it must run *before* the `enqueued()` comparison, or a tail whose only output is the flushed move looks like an empty level and is never drained). This also makes the move execute immediately rather than one command late as the 2.9 sequence would. **Test:** new `tests/mdi-feed-move` — plain MDI feed move, a second one (proving the first was emitted rather than deferred and swept out), and a feed move in an M66-queue-buster o-word sub tail; **each fix mutation-verified separately** (disabling the `executeMDI` flush fails the plain case at `position=(0,0)`; disabling the `finishMDI` flush fails only the sub-tail case). **Note on the test's own trap, since it cost a round:** the first draft used `G4` as the sub's queue buster, and dwell is **not** one — only probe/M66/M6/user-M set the flags that make `execute_block` return `INTERP_EXECUTE_FINISH` (`interp_execute.cc`) — so that sub ran entirely in the entry execution and silently re-tested the plain path, passing against the mutation. **Coverage gap that let this ship:** no test in the suite MDI'd a plain feed move and asserted the resulting motion — every MDI test either sets a mode, calls an M-code, or moves G0, and `tests/interp/mdi-oword-m66` puts a **G0** in the sub tail. **Behaviour note:** the flush also ends naive-CAM chaining *across* MDI lines, which a `G64 Q<n>` config could do in 2.9 — no regression (the chain was being dropped anyway), but it is a deliberate divergence. Verified: `make stmak-check` 0 issues; the most-affected clusters (`tests/abort` incl. `tests/abort/g64`, the naive-CAM parity test, + `tests/interp/mdi-oword-m66` + `tests/interp/oword-mdi-sub-update` + `tests/mdi-queue` + `tests/single-step` + the new test) 13/13; **full local runtests 254/254 successful, 0 failed, 0 xfail, 0 skipped**; and end-to-end from the ported CLI — `mdi g1 f600 x5 y5`, the exact command that moved nothing, now lands at (5, 5). |
| 2026-07-31 | **`mdi` and `teach-in` ported to gmi; the rest of the command-line surface surveyed** (branch `migrate-cmdline-tools`). A survey of everything in `bin/`+`scripts/` found the machine-API breakage confined to three non-UI tools — the removed `linuxcnc.stat()`/`command()` raise on construction, so `mdi`, `teach-in` and `debuglevel` had been dead since the emcmodule gutting; every other CLI is either already on gmi (`linuxcnctop`, `manualtoolchange_ui`), Go (`halcmd`, `halsampler`, `halstreamer`, `modcompile`, `ethercat`), INI-only (`inivar`, `lintini`, `update_ini`, `qtplasmac_gcode`), or independent of the machine (`axis-remote` drives a running AXIS over Tk `send`; `rs274`, `image-to-gcode` are filters). **`mdi`:** ported to `gmi.Command`, plus two things it could not do before — the upstream `eval(input(...))` left over from the 2to3 conversion meant the interactive prompt raised on any G-code line (broken since the py3 port, in 2.9 too), and a refused line said nothing at all. It now reports the *reason*: stratuMAK answers `POST /mdi` with an RCS status only, so the tool reads the shared operator-message list (never acknowledging, so a running GUI still sees everything) and prints what the command produced — refusal reasons, `(MSG,…)`, `(DEBUG,…)`. Exit status is a verdict, and deliberately not the RCS status alone: **a line issued while the interpreter is busy is queued, so the POST answers RCS_DONE and the line only fails later when the queue drains** — an error-kind message is therefore as much a verdict as the status is. **`teach-in`:** ported to `gmi.Stat().machine_units()` (the recorded coordinates must be in machine units, as classic reported them; the wire is mm), the dead `nmlfile` argument dropped for a plain optional output file, the boolean flags re-normalized to the documented `0`/`1` output format, and — new, and needed once the source is a network client — `poll()` failure no longer teaches a stale reading: `Stat.poll()` keeps the last snapshot on a failed fetch, so both labels now read `(not connected)` and "Learn" records nothing. Verified against a live `axis_mm.ini` sim: one-shot and interactive sessions, refusal/interp-error/`(MSG,…)`/queued-failure paths, dead-server paths, and teach-in driven headless under Xvfb in both joint and world mode. Man pages updated (`mdi.1` synopsis/exit status, both with `STMAK_REST_URL`/`STMAK_TASK_INSTANCE`). **NOT done, surfaced by the same survey and still open:** (a) the whole Tcl layer is dead — `tcl/hal.so` (from the deleted `halsh.c`) and `tcl/linuxcnc.so` (from `emcsh.cc`) no longer exist, so `haltcl`, `halreport`, `sim_pin`, `simulate_probe`, `hal-histogram`, `moveoff_gui`, `monitor-xhc-hb04`, TkLinuxCNC and `lib/hallib/*.tcl` are all still **built and installed** while failing on `package require Hal` (`.tcl` HALFILEs are already rejected outright by `internal/halfile/halfile.go:262`); (b) **`make install` fails** — `src/Makefile:709` still installs `../tcl/linuxcnc.so`, whose build rule was deleted with `emcsh.cc` (RIP builds never hit it); (c) `README.md` still documents `./scripts/linuxcnc <ini>` as the way to start, but that wrapper was removed in `135843fdf3`; (d) `debuglevel` left unported — recommend discarding it with the Tcl set. **Merge note (2026-08-03):** (b) and (d) were settled independently the same day on `stmak` — `debuglevel` deleted and `linuxcnctop` ported to a web app (see "The last two AXIS Tk tools resolved" above), and `../tcl/linuxcnc.so` removed from `src/Makefile` by the webapp-packaging sweep, which reached the same line from the other side. What survives this branch's side of that pair is the README launch commands, which the packaging work did not touch. (a) and (c) stand. |

| 2026-07-29 | **A G-code location is (file, line) — the whole chain, plus program filtering moved into the controller.** (`3c1a615e6d`, `4e28f475f3`, `7af82341be`, `7e61ead988`, `f15cded232`, `d6b62d6d0d`, `7c2725067d`, `b4eaa1dde3`, `15e236f0f2`.) Started as a field-reported regression: **every run of the same program highlighted a different, drifting set of lines in AXIS.** Cause was finding **A-15**, now **withdrawn as invalid — its "fix" was the bug**: it restored classic's `set_current_line(stat.motion_id or stat.motion_line)`, whose premise (motion id == line number, `emctaskmain.cc` `emcTrajSetMotionId(currentLine)`) stopped holding at `f9e48850b3`, where the id became an opaque serial plus a side table. `Canon.nextSerial` is never reset, so the first run's serials land in plausible line-number range and every later run drifts further. **The generalisation (A-18):** an o-word call into a separate file restarts the interpreter's line numbering (`interp_o_word.cc` `control_back_to`), and nothing on the wire said which file a line belonged to — so the highlight pointed at an unrelated line for the whole excursion, preview segments from a sub collided with the main program's identically-numbered lines, and `max_line` counted every file (measured: 19 for a 6-line program). Classic 2.9 is equally file-blind (`emctaskmain.cc:2199/2615`, `glcanon.py` `highlight`), so this is **new capability, not parity repair**. Wire: `task.motion_file` + `task.source_file` in `emcstat.gmi`; `PreviewResult.files[]` with `file_idx`/`seq` on Segment/Dwell/ToolChange in `ngcpreview.gmi`. **The defect only a running machine showed:** the naive-CAM chain flushes while a LATER line executes, so asking the interpreter for the file at flush time filed every move at a call boundary under the wrong side of it — right line, wrong file. Unit tests and two code-reading passes missed it; a live (file, line) trace found it in one run. The file is now captured into `chainedPt` beside the line number it belongs to, exactly as the state tag already was, and `allocSerialAt` makes that the rule for any future deferred emitter. **API changes made rather than worked around** (maintainer ruling: "change the api where necessary, we want a clean solution"): (a) **canon `dwell(lineno, seconds)` / `change_tool(lineno, slot)`** — a **deliberate departure from classic `emccanon`**, which passes neither; before it, those events inherited whatever moved last, and stratuMAK's `CanonState.lineNo` existed only to feed the tool-change traverse that stale number (deleted with its five writes). The sai oracle's printed format is unchanged (it never printed line numbers for motion either), so `tests/interp` shows zero churn. (b) **`pathres.Canonical`** — absolute, cleaned, symlink-resolved — applied to the loaded program, `motion_file` and the preview's file table alike, so comparing two program paths answers "the same file?"; AXIS takes the program's identity from `stat.file` instead of the path it sent. (c) **`GLCanon` records a location id** (index into `canon.locations` of (file, line)) in the slot classic used for the line number: the parallel `*_files` lists are gone, the C drawers and `calc_extents` are untouched because the tuple shape is unchanged, and **GL picking became file-aware for free** since `glLoadName()` names that slot. **N-7 CLOSED** — every preview item carries `seq` from one recorder counter and `gcode.parse` merges on it, so no line-number ordering assumption remains (the old line-number walk only held while line numbers increased; an O-word loop or a called file broke it). Nesting depth was checked and is **not** a gap: the interpreter keeps filename+sequence per call frame and restores on return, so both mechanisms report the file execution is *in*, at any depth — measured through two levels and both unwinds. **Filtering moved server-side** (maintainer ruling): a `[FILTER]` source is not G-code, and classic left the conversion to each GUI — a UI without filtering could not open those programs, and the controller's own `[DISPLAY]OPEN_FILE` handed the raw source to the interpreter, failing on line 1. `program_open` now runs the converter and opens its output; `file` is the G-code executed, `source_file` what the operator opened. It **cannot** run inline (image-to-gcode takes minutes), so it runs on a goroutine with `filtering`/`filter_progress` in stat (classic `FILTER_PROGRESS` protocol, so existing filters report progress unchanged), the previously loaded program stays runnable until the new one is ready, a failure reports the converter's own stderr on the **operator error channel**, and abort cancels it. Output lives in `pathres.FilteredDir()` (`/tmp/stmak-filtered-<pid>`), a program root so `get_file`/`gen_preview` can read it — pid-derived because the task and ngcpreview are separate modules in one process. **AXIS lost 99 lines of filtering**; the async adoption exposed a startup path that claimed `stat.file` as "the program we are showing" without its text on screen, so the resync saw no change and the program never appeared — found by instrumenting the running UI, not by reading the diff. **New security surface, see the cross-cutting item:** the controller now execs a user-configured program. `internal/programfilter` runs it with **no shell** (argv split here, the file name passed as its own argument — classic's `sh -c "%s '%s'"` let a file name containing a quote run commands, and there is a test for exactly that name), its own process group killed on **`[FILTER]FILTER_TIMEOUT`**, and the real uid restored if euid differs; the child inherits none of stmakd's file capabilities. **Tests:** three new suites — `tests/motion-file-trace` (the real stack, asserts the whole (file, line) sequence across two call levels, that `motion_file == stat.file == preview files[0]`, and that a symlinked spelling comes back as one identity), `tests/program-filter` (13 assertions: non-blocking open, progress, converted-vs-source, readable/previewable output, the converter got the right source, failure keeps the loaded program and reports its words, no partial program left behind), `tests/axis-subfile-highlight` (headless glcanon: location interning, colliding-line ids, pick resolution) — plus `internal/programfilter`, `pathres` canonicalisation, task filtering-state and preview event-order unit tests. **Mutation-verified**, each against the defect it exists for: reverting the naive-CAM capture turns `main.ngc:3` into `trace_sub.ngc:3`; making the open wait for the conversion fails with the measured `2.00s`; ordering the replay by line number puts the dwell at the program's final position; dropping the file from `highlight()` lights up both files' line 2. **Residuals → follow-ups #19/#20.** |
| 2026-07-27 | **Meson retired; its three unit suites migrated into `stmak-test` — and porting them found a tool-offset corruption bug on the real machine.** (`1e18a37962`, `a206582a3c`, `fcd546a308`, `6809d674d0`, `846ee24cd8`; CI green incl. full runtests.) `meson.build` was a second, standalone build system — `project('linuxcnc-unit-test')` — whose only purpose was `unit_tests/`. Nothing referenced it: not autotools, not `debian/`, not CI. Its last substantive upstream commit was 2019-06-28, it still required python2 + boost-python, and on stratuMAK it also `subdir()`'d the deleted `src/emc/pythonplugin`, so it had been unbuildable for years. With CMake ahead there was no reason to carry it; **14 `meson.build` files + `unit_tests/` deleted**, `greatest.h` moved next to its only remaining user, `catch.hpp` dropped, `debian/copyright` and `src/rtapi/README` followed. **All three suites now run under `make stmak-test`** (PR gate + nightly race build) instead of in a build nobody could run. **(1) interp** — the C++/Catch suite had not compiled since the embedded-Python removal (`tests_main.cc` still includes `python_plugin.hh`); all **21 cases** are re-expressed in Go against the milltask interp shim, driving the REAL interpreter with the REAL Go canon over a recording motion sink. That drops the old harness's global-state dance (`reset_internals`/`_outfile`/delete-and-recreate, which existed only because saicanon is global) and means a shim regression now fails the tests — the C++ suite linked the class directly and could stay green with the ABI milltask actually uses broken underneath it. New shim accessors (`interp_get_parameter`, `interp_length_units`, `interp_origin_index`, `interp_current_{position,work_offset,axis_offset}`) go through `interp_inspection.hh`, the documented seam, not raw `_setup` fields. **(2) halscope RT** — 26 tests, now a cgo package (`internal/halscope/testrt`, separate from the parent so the `#include`d `halscope_rt.c` does not collide). It had **never once run**: two drift fixes were needed just to compile — `RTAPI_NONBLOCKING` undefined (testmock now includes the real `rtapi_rt_check.h` rather than stubbing it, so the annotation stays live under a clang that supports `-Wfunction-effects`) and `rtapi_calloc`/`rtapi_free` missing since halscope moved to the RT-safe allocator. **(3) blendmath** — rewritten natively in Go (`internal/tptest`); the predicates are unreachable otherwise (built into `motmod.so`, which exports nothing, and there is no `libtp`), so the four-file link closure is `#include`d and `-lposemath` linked. **The bug (`846ee24cd8`): queued tool-table writes were invisible to the interpreter's own reads, losing G10 offsets.** The interp keeps only slot 0 resident (`load_tool_table`) and fetches other tools on demand via `find_tool_index`, which **caches the canon's answer back into `settings->tool_table[idx]`**; 2.9 instead bulk-loaded the whole table per synch and never re-read in between. The write meanwhile stayed queued (`SetToolTableEntry` → `SetToolTableEntryCmd` → `io.ToolSetOffset` → store), so a read between the two saw the pre-write entry and the cache-fill overwrote the interpreter's own uncommitted edit: `g10 l1 p1 x1` followed by `g10 l1 p1 y2` **silently reverted X to 0**. AUTO reads ahead without draining unless a line returns EXECUTE_FINISH, and G10 L1 does not — so this corrupted tool offsets on iron, not just in tests. Fixed by writing through to the layer the reader consults: a pending-write overlay in the canon (read-modify-write, as iocontrol's `gmi_tool_set_offset` does, so `pocketno` and `comment` survive), served by `GetExternalToolTable`/`GetToolByNumber`, dropped in `SetToolTableEntryCmd.PostWait` once the store is current, cleared by `InitCanon`. **Deliberately not** fixed by writing the store at enqueue time (the interp runs far ahead of execution — that would make a G10 in an aborted program permanent, where 2.9 discards it; tool offsets are persistent machine state) nor by restoring the bulk load (whose cost is why the lazy read exists). **Three findings about the old suite as an oracle.** Its expected values are *not* trustworthy: the G52 case's `1.0` silently encoded an **inch machine**, because saicanon's `_external_length_units` is set in `driver.cc`, which the Catch harness never linked — the expectation rode on an uninitialised global. Both machines are now pinned (metric → 25.4, inch → 1.0), and the crossing is isolated with an explicit `G21` rather than a startup default. Separately, **G20-on-an-inch-machine start was re-verified as CORRECT** (2.9's `INIT_CANON` derives `canon.lengthUnits` from `[TRAJ]LINEAR_UNITS` identically; `72cc319893` moved stratuMAK *to* that behavior) — not a bug, do not "fix" it. And two 2019 `FIXME this is the wrong behavior but what the interpreter currently expects` judgements are carried over verbatim: the tests pin current behavior so a change is visible, they do not endorse it. **Two more traps caught.** cgo silently evaluates a *composed* macro (`TP_ANGLE_EPSILON_SQ` = `EPS*EPS`) to **0** instead of failing — with every predicate a `< tol`, that turned all positive assertions false while the negatives still passed, reading like a maths bug; the epsilons are now real C constants with `TestAngleEpsilonsAreReal` guarding it. And `stmak-test-race` caught an unlocked test double (`fakeToolTable`) — that concurrency is real (interp thread reads, sequencer writes; the production store uses an `RWMutex`), so the fake does too. **Verification:** CI green incl. the full runtests suite; locally `runtests tests/{tlo,tool-info,toolchanger,t0,rs274ngc-startup,io-startup}` 18/18, full `stmak-test` 0 failures, `stmak-test-race`, `stmak-lint` 0 issues, `stmak-fmt-check`, full `make`. Follow-ups #17/#18 opened (non-standard-units diagnostic; the two deliberately-dropped interp pieces). |

| 2026-07-24 | **Pre-merge review sweep of the post-review commits (stratuMAK merge gate) — 4 parallel adversarial passes over everything after `0fc51d25af`, 13 fixes landed, 6 commits.** Scope: the ~20 substantive commits that landed after the Phase-6 review coverage ended (runtests-green but never review-swept), clustered as HAL/RT core (period-constraint drops, newthread/CPU pool, error-reason ABI, EtherCAT bump), tool-table slot re-key, multi-instance `/info`, and AXIS/error-surface (pathres whitelist, operator-error channel, `_watch`). Every finding adjudicated against the code before fixing. **Fixed (each with tests, mutation-verified where the shape allows):** ① **iotask HIGH** — the slot re-key made the non-random spindle's slot-0 copy *durable*, so a restart served the pre-restart tool from slot 0 while io reported the spindle empty: a bare `G43`/`G43 H0` applied a phantom tool-length offset (2.9's `.tbl` never stored that row). First fixed as an iocontrol startup reset — **REDESIGNED by ruling 2026-07-24**: correcting bad state is weaker than making it unrepresentable, so the non-random slot 0 is now **session state in the tooltable module** (in-memory, never persisted — exactly where 2.9 solved it, in the persistence layer via `tooldata_save` starting at idx 1); a non-random Start also *purges* any persisted slot-0 row (protects the RANDOM_TOOLCHANGER config-flip case), the C resets are reverted (iocontrol is a pure 2.9 port again, no start-order dependence), and boot does zero corrective writes. Random unchanged: slot 0 is pocket 0 and must persist. Multi-spindle checked against 2.9: the tool subsystem there is single-tool-spindle by construction (one scalar `toolInSpindle`, one idx-0, one handshake) — per-spindle tools would be a task/io/interp-wide feature belonging to the deferred table/changer-state split, which this model eases (N in-memory spindle rows, not N persisted pseudo-slots). Two-boot regression `tests/io-startup/nonrandom/restart-empty-spindle` (asserts behavior, survived the redesign) + store tests for session/persist/purge semantics. ② **milltask HIGH** — gating the AXIS pyvcp panel on the `/info` peer silently killed the panel of the migrated demo config (nothing in-tree sets `pyvcp_instance=`; milltask loads from the LIB-shared hal file). First fixed by deriving the peer from `[DISPLAY]PYVCP` in milltask — **REVERTED by ruling** (`2adba99c9f`): that key was just declared dead and no special handling belongs in code. Resolved in the template instead: `LIB:linuxcnc.hal` fills `pyvcp_instance=` on the milltask load line from an optional `[TASK]PYVCP_INSTANCE` key (the same mechanism as the per-joint `HOMEMOD` line), the demo config sets it; key-less configs render byte-identically, and the panel remains a load-line fact. Classic POSTGUI pyvcp configs stay unmigrated content for the config-migration pass. Verified end-to-end: `GET /info` on the booted demo reports the peer. ③ **task MED** — tools-API concurrent creates were handed the same free slot (check-then-act over `NextFreeIndex`; a create's stamp 0 bypasses the CAS) → second create silently destroyed the first tool; `writeMu` serializes PutTool/DeleteTool. ④ **gmi MED** — `taskinfo` latched *transport* failures forever (connection-refused at startup froze `Stat.tool_table` empty for the session, contradicting the lazy-resolution entitlement documented the same day); only a 404 is latched now. ⑤ **task MED** — `ensureMode` refusals in `autoCommand`/`Home`/`OverrideLimits` (the preflight-vs-body race window) and the estop-reset refusal were message-less after the sole-error-surface change → operator clicks died silently; all message now, as MDI's body already did. ⑥ **halcmd MED** — failed-newthread rollback restored a whole-pool snapshot, resurrecting cores acquired by *concurrent* calls (auto-assign then co-locates a thread onto a live exclusive core); replaced by a per-acquisition lease/release. Plus: online-CPU detection from sysfs (NumCPU is the affinity-mask size, not the top index), termios restore on external kill at the raw-mode prompt, a 2 s timeout on TAB-completion queries (unkillable-from-keyboard hang on a hung server), comment-tolerant input validation (Latin-1 comments in legacy HAL files), `ProgramOpen` "no such file" vs "not allowed" wording (`pathres.ErrNotFound`), the nc_files program root's first reason-asserting containment tests, two `_watch` exit races (CancelledError escaping a `finally`, pending-task warning at loop close), the multi-instance command test tightened to a bare `Command()` (it passed pre-fix), and four misleading comments. **Clean after adversarial refutation:** the rtapi/hal_lib period-constraint removals' call-path audit, the `hal_*_ex` ABI fan-out (no missed caller, bounded buffers), the EtherCAT master IRQ-pinning bump, the pathres nc_files root (canonicalized, symlink-safe, fail-closed), the operator-error consolidation's startup/disconnect windows (retained list + snapshot-on-subscribe lose nothing), the keepalive ping, and both flake fixes (genuine infra, assertions intact). **Left open for adjudication, deliberately not fixed:** ~~an absolute thread-period floor~~ — **RULED + FIXED same day** (`b5d0c1e994`): `HAL_MIN_PERIOD_NSEC` 1 µs in `hal_create_thread_cpu_ex` + `halcmd.gmi` `@min(1000)`, refusal names the nanosecond unit; remapped-T `stdglue` still feeding the carousel pocket into `selected_pocket` (bug-for-bug 2.9 parity — the commit fixed the identical conflation in `settool_epilog` only); a store format/version marker (a pre-re-key dev DB mis-keys silently); the C `hal_create_thread_cpu_ex` late-failure paths that leave a live thread the pool then reuses; tooledit multi-row legend clipping. Gate: `make stmak-lint` 0 / fmt clean, six touched packages `-race` green, 18 runtests across the tool clusters + io-startup + pyvcp + multi-instance + gmi-shim + tooledit green. |
| 2026-07-23 | **Multi-instance UI: clients stopped guessing peer names and instances — `GET /info` added, found via a real two-task machine config.** A UI client has to know the name of every module it addresses, and it had no way to learn them, so it *derived* them — the ngcpreview instance from its own name (`f"{instance}-preview"`), the raw tool-table store from a hardcoded `"tooltable"`. Both are correct only when there is exactly one of everything; with `STMAK_TASK_INSTANCE=pnp.task` the store is `pnp.tt`, so `stat.tool_table` 404'd, and because a failed fetch is not cached while axis.py reads `tool_table[0]` once per display cycle, one wrong name became **~10 failed requests a second for the session**. The same guess ran through the capability probes: AXIS asked HAL whether `spindle.0.forward` had a writer to decide whether to show the spindle controls, but the real pin is `pnp.mot.spindle.0.forward` (only milltask knows the `pnp.mot` part), so the probes matched nothing and **silently hid the spindle, coolant and limit-override controls** (~26 REST round-trips to reach the wrong answer). And the command channel itself: `AxisCommand` subclasses `gmi.command.Command`, whose instance defaulted to the literal `"milltask"`, and it was built bare — so on a multi-instance server **every command (jog-speed, state, program-open, …) 404'd** while stat kept working (it routes through the `gmi.Stat()` factory that applies `instance()`). **Fix — one endpoint for the missing facts:** milltask serves `GET /api/v1/{task}/info` → `TaskPeers` (tooltable/preview/manualtoolchange/pyvcp — only the modules a client addresses directly; motion/io/persist/halui stay milltask's business) + `TaskCaps` (what is *wired*, asked of HAL not the INI — 2.9 showed the spindle controls only when a pin was driven; flags kept one-per-pin, `spindle_forward`/`reverse`/`on`/`speed`/`brake`/`coolant_mist`/`coolant_flood`/`limit_switch_override`, not a collapsed verdict, so a lathe wiring one direction still gets one button; spindle/joint pins qualified with the motion instance, coolant with io). Peer names come from new `load milltask` params (`preview_instance=`/`mtc_instance=`/`pyvcp_instance=`, not another convention) that **Start resolves by `api:instance`** — so `preview_instance=pnp.tt` is rejected and a typo **fails the config load** where the operator is looking, not a client 404 much later; unset = feature absent = client skips it (retires the last legitimate startup 404, the MTC probe). `GetInfo` refuses before Start rather than handing back a plausible-empty default, because clients read it once and cache it. **Client (`gmi`):** `gmi.info()` fetches once and **caches the failure too** (a server too old, a wrong `STMAK_TASK_INSTANCE`, a task that never started — none heal on retry, and retrying is how the storm happened); `preview_instance()`/`mtc_instance()`/`pyvcp_instance()`/`tooltable_instance()` resolve from it, the derived guess deleted; `Stat` floors a *failed* tool-table refetch at 1/s (the fetch failure must not be cached or the table freezes, so nothing else bounded it) and resolves the store lazily so a driver may still construct a `Stat` before the server is up. **Uniform instance schema:** one rule (`gmi.resolve_instance` — explicit wins, unset follows `STMAK_TASK_INSTANCE`) now used by `Command`/`Stat`/`ErrorChannel`/`MessageList`/`ToolTable`, so a directly-constructed client can no longer reopen the `"milltask"` trap; single-instance behaviour is byte-identical (unset → `instance()` → `"milltask"`). **AXIS:** the pin probes + 16-joint limit loop collapse into the one `/info` fetch (~26 startup round-trips → 1), the panel instance follows `pyvcp_instance()`, the command channel binds to `gmi.instance()`. **New `tests/multi-instance`** — two deliberately asymmetric task stacks (left: forward+speed+mist; right: reverse-only+flood+one limit switch) on one server, so reading the wrong instance yields a *different* answer, not an accidental match; a `config-is-a-reproducer` guard asserts no `tooltable`/`milltask`/`ngcpreview` default name resolves in it (else a hardcoding client would pass and the test be theatre); the preview instances are deliberately **not** `<task>-preview` (the first draft followed that convention and passed with the derived guess restored). **Verified by mutation** (hardcoded `tooltable`, restored derived preview name, dropped instance prefixes, and the bare `"milltask"` command each fail it by name); `tests/gmi-shim` extended with the instance-resolution schema + `/info` contract cases; `make stmak-check` 0 issues, `make` clean incl. the regenerated C/Go/Python clients, **local runtests 243/243**, **confirmed working on the real machine**, CI runtests running. Follow-on to the Phase-6 AXIS/gmi rows. **Two follow-ups left open** (not blocking): making an unknown `load milltask` arg a hard error (would have caught a no-op `db=…` arg on a real machine config), and whether each task should get its own pyvcp panel rather than sharing one. |
| 2026-07-23 | **Tool table re-keyed by SLOT — 2.9's tooldata model, ported onto persist** (`920bfb085e`; reported as sittner/linuxcnc#272). The store had been keyed by TOOL NUMBER, and nearly every tool-handling defect followed from that one difference: keyed by toolno **the spindle record has nowhere to live**. iocontrol still wrote it — under key 0, colliding with a real T0 entry — and `put_tool` clobbered the stored toolno with the key, so the record always read back as tool 0. That is why the canon could not use it (#5400 stuck at 0 after M6; see the superseded lifecycle-sweep bullet above) and reconstructed the spindle from `toolInSpindle` plus a last-known-good snapshot held in the `Canon`; why a phantom tool 0 appeared in every listing and in the tooledit UI; and why ngcpreview previewed a G43-opening program with no offset. **Worse, slot and pocket had been conflated, and that was corrupting operator data:** G10 L1/L10/L11 calls `set_tool_table_entry` twice when the edited tool is the loaded one, the second time with idx 0 to update the spindle copy (`interp_convert.cc:4129`); `gmi_tool_set_offset` built a fresh `memset` entry and stored it under the tool number, so that second call wrote `pocketno=0` onto the real tool's row — **a tool touch-off in AXIS silently destroyed the tool's home pocket**, and every G10 dropped the tool's comment. 2.9 read-modify-writes (`tooldata_get` → assign → `tooldata_put`, comments in a parallel `ttcomments[idx]` array); so do we now. **The store therefore takes slots** (slot 0 = spindle, 1..N = the table; keys zero-padded so sqlite's `ORDER BY key` is numeric). Slot 0 is materialised at startup and cannot be deleted, which **is** #272: `stat.tool_table` is a dense slot-indexed list again, so `tool_table[0]` is the spindle and never raises on a config with no tools — axis.py, touchy and gscreen all subscript it unguarded (verified live: an empty table now yields a one-entry table with an empty spindle instead of `[]`). New store calls `find_index_for_tool` (ports `tooldata_find_index_for_tool`, incl. the rule that a tool's own slot beats the spindle's copy of it) and `next_free_index`; the `.tbl` import assigns slots 2.9's way (P **is** the slot on a random changer; a fakepocket counter from 1 on a non-random one, with P kept as the carousel pocket). **Canon/interp:** `get_external_tool_table` and `get_tool_by_number` return slot **and** pocket separately, so `find_tool_index` caches at the slot instead of at the carousel pocket while `find_tool_pocket` still reports the pocket; M61 sends the slot again — the builtin did before the port, and stdglue's remap now does too via a new `find_tool_index` interp-ctx callback (2.9's *Python* stdglue passed the pocket there, leaving `#<_current_pocket>` on the wrong row — a 2.9 bug, deliberately not reproduced). `emcio.pocket_prepped` is an idx again, as classic stat documented it ("the index into the stat.tool_table list"), which also retires the per-status-poll sqlite lookup that stood in for it and the `Canon` spindle snapshot. **Scope of the wire change:** only the raw `/api/v1/tooltable/` store became slot-addressed (entries gain `idx`); the operator-facing milltask `tools` API stays tool-number addressed and resolves to a slot server-side, so the tooledit app is unaffected. On a **random** changer slot 0 is pocket 0 — a real, editable row, not a copy — so it is listed and editable there and hidden only where it duplicates a tool that has its own row; creating a new tool there now requires a free pocket (the pocket **is** the slot) instead of silently overwriting the occupant. **No migration written** (user waiver: the previous stratuMAK format was on no machine). **Two ported tests asserted the pre-port semantics and were corrected to 2.9's**, not the code: `toolchanger/reload-tool/non-random` expects tool-table **indices** for `pocket_prepped` (2.9's own comment: "ugh, non-random tc gives you tool-table-array index, not pocket"), and `tooledit/test.py` counted the spindle slot as a tool. Gates: build/vet/gofmt clean, all Go tests + 85 webapp tests green, new coverage for slot CRUD, the spindle invariant across restart, undeletable slot 0, `find_index_for_tool`/`next_free_index` for both changer kinds, `.tbl` slot assignment for both, and the task-layer tool-number↔slot translation incl. the random-changer path; **runtests 242 run / 241 successful** — the one failure (`abort/g64`) is the known motion-timing flake and passes in isolation. |

| 2026-07-22 | **`map[string]T` + `@watch_delta` land in the GMI IDL — pyvcp's `watch_state` is now generated, closing findings-doc D-2 and template condition (a) the day it was written.** The deferral's "large codegen feature" costing was stale: it dated from when REST went through the C dispatch, but after the Go-native handler switch (`9101c9ca4d`) the C ABI is the only place a map has no shape — and a watch never crosses it. So the feature is deliberately **narrow**: string keys only (a JSON object key IS a string; the parser rejects any other key), allowed *only* as the full return type of a watch-only func (`@watch`, no `@method`), no nested maps, no nullable values (a missing key already expresses absence) — every other placement (struct field, param, plain return, dual-purpose watch, map-in-slice) fails the checker with the reason. **The C-provider surface skips such a func entirely** — header typedef/vtable/`GMI_*_CALLBACKS` macro, cgo call wrapper, dispatch func, FuncMeta — with an emitted comment ("JSON-only watch (map return) — no C ABI, Go providers only"), so a C provider that tries to serve it fails at compile, not silently. **`@watch_delta true`** moves pyvcp's hand-set `Delta: true` (per-connection top-level-key diff) into the IDL; rejected on non-`@watch` funcs and on binary `[]u8` watches (no JSON keys to diff), and emitted only when set, so **every existing IDL's generated output is byte-identical** (no other IDL uses maps or `@watch_delta` — grep-verified — and `TestMapWatchDeltaOmittedWhenUnset` pins it). Type mappings: Go `map[string]T` (via the single `goTypeForDispatch`, honoring the four-copies lesson — the TS/Py/client-go copies each got the case too, the client-go one unreachable-but-mapped), TS `Record<string, T>` incl. the bigint reviver walking map values, Python `dict[str, T]` with per-value `from_dict` in the generated WS client. **pyvcp converted:** `pyvcp.gmi` declares `watch_state() -> map[string]WidgetState` `@watch_delta true`; `pyvcpmodule` implements the generated `PyvcpWatchCallbacks` (`WatchState() (map[string]pyvcp.WidgetState, error)`, closed-panel guard intact) and registers via `RegisterPyvcpWatch` — the manual `WatchAPI` block is deleted. Documented in `src/gmi/idl/README.md` (Type System row, "Maps", "`@watch_delta`") and `VCP_MIGRATION.md`. **Verified:** parser tests (map parse, non-string-key rejection), 9 checker cases (7 confinement + 2 `@watch_delta`), `cgen/map_watch_test.go` asserting all four surfaces (Go provider iface + `Delta: true`, C header/dispatch skip *and* ordinary funcs intact, TS/Py types); `go build ./...`, vet, gofmt clean; pyvcpmodule + gmicompile `-race` green; `stmak-lint-full` 0; **`tests/pyvcp` runtests green** — the untouched hand-written Python client passing against the generated registration proves the wire contract byte-compatible. |

| 2026-07-22 | **A refused command is a 409, not a 500** — the status contract for the REST surface, after the previous commit made provider errors reachable at all. `writeDispatchError` defaulted every un-errno'd error to **500**, and the task layer's refusals ("must be in MDI mode", "can't issue MDI command when not homed") are plain `fmt.Errorf`, so a machine correctly *declining* a command reported itself as a broken controller. That is not cosmetic: a 500 invites a retry against a machine presumed sick and is what monitoring escalates on. Fixed by making the provider state the *kind* of failure and the transport choose the status — `apiserver.Fault` with `FaultState` → **409 Conflict** (the machine's state forbids it; nothing happened, re-sending unchanged fails the same way), `FaultNotReady` → **503** (module not started or stopped; may succeed shortly), `FaultNotFound` → **404**, `FaultInternal` (the zero value, so unclassified errors keep the conservative 500) → **500**. `internal/task` classifies in one place: `rcFor` wraps an unclassified refusal as `FaultState`, an already-classified error keeps its kind (`errNotReady` stays 503), and all 34 command methods now route through it rather than the two that did. **Third defect found in passing:** the errno→status map used a bare `switch err`, so any provider that wrapped its errno for context — the normal thing to do — fell through to 500 anyway; it now uses `errors.Is`. The layering is deliberate: the task layer says what happened, not what HTTP status it deserves. Contract documented in `src/gmi/idl/README.md` ("Failure reporting"), including the rule that an *accepted-and-faulted* command is not a transport error at all. runtests 241/241; Go suite, vet, gofmt, golangci-lint clean. **The remaining four classified the same day**, each decided on its own merits rather than swept: `inirest`'s "INI file not loaded" → **409**, because an INI-less launcher (halrun mode) is *permanent* for that process and a 503 would invite a retry that can never succeed; its parameter-file failures (unset / unresolvable / unreadable) → **404**, because all three are one answer to a client — there is no parameter file — and the reason, including a containment refusal, still travels in the message for the operator debugging their INI; `persist_sqlite`'s invalid handle → **404** (a namespace never opened or already released); `emccalib`'s "not in tunable list" → **404** (the tunable list is the allow-list, so a key not on it does not exist as a tunable). `persist_sqlite`'s namespace cap got a **new kind, `FaultCapacity` → 503**, deliberately not reusing `FaultNotReady`: that module is running and healthy, it is simply full, and conflating the two would make the name lie in logs — they can also diverge later if one wants `Retry-After`. Mutation-verified (disabling the classification fails all four mapping tests; removing the errno unwrap fails the wrapped-errno one). |
| 2026-07-22 | **Audited every path-containment test by mutation; one was vacuous** (`internal/haljson`). The inirest pass had turned up a test that passed for the wrong reason, so the same question was put to the whole tree the only way that answers it: disable `pathres.contains()` — the containment check itself — and see which tests notice. **Five packages did** (`pathres`, `halfile`, `inirest`, `launcher`, `emccalib`); one that claims to did not. haljson's `TestNewHaljsonModuleErrors` case "config escaping the roots" used `config=../../etc/passwd` from a `t.TempDir()`, which reaches nothing — so it failed on "not found", asserted only `err != nil`, and would have passed with containment deleted outright. It was an exact duplicate of the "unresolvable config" case on the line above it. Fixed the way the inirest one was: config root and escape target as known siblings under one parent, a target that is real **and parseable** (so a containment failure builds a working module and fails the test loudly), and an assertion on the reason. Re-ran the mutation: six packages now catch it. Also added the reason assertion to emccalib's `TestSaveIniOutsideRoot` — it was already sound, because it asserts the out-of-root file is unchanged rather than merely that an error came back, but "any error will do" would equally pass on a typo'd path. **The other half of the question — modules that resolve but then use the unvalidated input — came back clean:** all nine `pathres.Resolve` call sites (`adsmodule`, `classicladder` ×3, `haljson`, `mqttbridge`, `persist_sqlite`, `pyvcpmodule`, `task`, `tooltable`) shadow the input variable with the resolved path, which makes using the unchecked value impossible rather than merely unlikely, and `PATH_RESOLUTION_INVENTORY.md` has categories A/B/C all done. **The transferable rule:** a containment test proves nothing unless the escape target actually exists and is reachable by the path under test, *and* the test asserts why the refusal happened — otherwise "not found" masquerades as "refused". Independent `t.TempDir()` calls are not predictably siblings, which is what made both of these miss. |
| 2026-07-22 | **I-3 fixed in gmi — `string?` is genuinely nullable now, and that closed a live EtherCAT bug** (ruling + write-up in `NETWORK_MODULES_REVIEW_FINDINGS.md`). Maintainer ruling: fix the emitter rather than delete inirest's dead branch, because a construct the IDL offers but a provider cannot express is a gap in gmi. **The severity was decided by what the fix investigation found.** `ethercat.gmi`'s `EoeIpRequest` has six `string?` fields and the C provider is written to exactly the documented contract — `gmi_ethercat.c` is a run of `if (req->mac_address)`, `if (req->hostname)`, where NULL means "not supplied, leave the device alone". But the REST dispatch is Go and `GoToC` emitted `C.CString("")` for an absent field, so every one of those checks was **true for a field the caller never sent**. Five survived by luck (`sscanf`/`inet_pton` reject `""`, leaving `*_included` clear); `hostname` has no parse step, so it took the empty name and set `name_included = 1` — **an EoE IP request setting only `ip_address` also wiped the slave's hostname**. `master_index: u32?` in the same call was correctly a NULL pointer, which is the proof only strings were broken. **Root cause: four copies of one rule.** `serverGoGen.toGoType`, `goTypeForDispatch`, `clientGoGen.toGoType` and `constraintEmitter.goIsPointer` each independently decided whether a type maps to a Go pointer, and three carried a `t.Name != PrimString` exception — so `string?` was `*string` in the standalone Go client and a plain `string` everywhere a provider could see it. Fixed by making `goTypeForDispatch` the single mapping (the server copy delegates, `goIsPointer` agrees) plus the four converter directions that follow: `CToGo` keeps NULL as nil, `GoToC` leaves the pointer NULL for nil (struct fields *and* parameters), and the provider bridge hands nil to the implementation for a NULL argument. Writing the emission test caught a bug in that last one — the generated nil check tested the Go variable rather than the incoming C pointer, so a nullable param would always have arrived nil. **Two things fell out:** `[]string?` binds the `?` to the *element* (a slice of nullable strings — never what anyone meant, and identical code to `[]string` while nullable strings were demoted); all three uses meant "optional slice", which needs no marker, and the checker now rejects it. And `@notnull` on a `string?` had been rejected with the reason "(string? maps to a non-pointer Go string)" — the bug, quoted — so it is now allowed. **Kept as `string?` deliberately:** the 12 *parameters* (`pattern`, `level`, `type`, `namespace`, `kind`), where empty and absent really are the same; demoting them would cost the generated TS/Python clients their optional-argument affordance, and one uniform rule beats 11 saved nil-checks. Guidance on which to use is now in `src/gmi/idl/README.md`. **This changes the REST wire, unlike G-1/G-2, and that is the point:** a present-but-empty value encodes as `{"value":""}` instead of being indistinguishable from absent. `inirest.Query` needed no logic change — its `keyExists` branch simply became reachable. Five mutations on the emitters/checker all caught; EtherCAT regression test on the wire contract; halscope dispatch test that an omitted optional parameter reaches the provider as nil. build/vet/gofmt/golangci-lint (0 issues)/`-race` clean, whole Go suite green. Full runtests owed at the Phase-5 checkpoint. |
| 2026-07-22 | **inirest `U` closed — 57.5 → 100 %, and the last Phase-5 coverage row with it** (`NETWORK_MODULES_REVIEW_FINDINGS.md`, "inirest coverage pass"). The entire gap was `GetParameterFile` at **0 %** — the one method in the package that touches the disk, taking a path out of operator-supplied INI content and returning that file's *contents* over REST, with a `pathres` containment check as the only thing between `[RS274NGC]PARAMETER_FILE = ../../etc/shadow` and any REST caller. Now covered: containment refusal for an absolute *and* a relative escape, plus that an in-root traversal still resolves (so the check cannot degenerate into "reject anything with `..`"); namespace override and global fallback on both methods; and the three failures that must stay distinct rather than collapsing into an empty string — PARAMETER_FILE unset, file missing, file unreadable — because an empty parameter table is a plausible-looking answer. Five mutations, all caught. **Method note worth carrying:** the first containment test passed for the wrong reason — two independent `t.TempDir()` calls are not predictably siblings, so the traversal missed and the refusal was an ordinary "not found"; asserting only `err != nil` would have stayed green with containment deleted outright. Any path-containment test that does not assert *why* it was refused is suspect. **New finding I-3 (open, needs a ruling):** a `string?` result field cannot express "not found", because the two Go emitters disagree — `cgen/server_go.go` `toGoType` *explicitly* excludes strings from the nullable-pointer rule (`t.Name != ast.PrimString`) so the **provider** sees a plain `string` with `omitempty`, while `cgen/client_go.go` maps the same field to `*string`. So `ini.gmi`'s documented "null if not found" is unrepresentable server-side: `Query`'s absent-key branch produces `IniQueryResult{}` and its present-but-empty branch produces `{Value:""}`, both marshal to exactly `{"values":null}`, and the `keyExists` call distinguishing them is dead. `bool?` is `*bool` on both sides, which is why sibling field `all` works. The generated Python client is built for the documented contract (`Optional[str]`, `d.get("value", None)`) and can never receive the distinction. 24 `string?` fields across four IDLs; result *fields* are where it bites. Deliberately not fixed inside a coverage pass — aligning the emitters moves a generated Go type in every API that uses `string?`, the same blast radius that correctly routed G-1/G-2 through a ruling. Pinned meanwhile by a test asserting the *actual* wire behaviour. |

The network pass killed that reason — the keep-alive `TestMain` pattern runs a real in-process HAL inside the test binary — and emccalib is a bad module to leave uncovered on a technicality, because *all four* of its REST methods exist to move numbers between HAL pins and the operator's INI file, and one of them (`SaveIni`) rewrites that file in place. One pre-existing test-harness bug fixed while there: the helpers registered the API under `t.Name()`, but registration is process-global and never unregistered, so the package could not run under `-count=2` at all — now uniquified, and green under `-race -count=3`. `calibreg` was already 100 %. vet/gofmt/golangci-lint clean. |
| 2026-07-22 | **REST address conflicts now fail fast — in the server and in runtests** (`bed454f529`). A port conflict was the most expensive failure mode this suite had: not silent, but loud in the wrong place. One `stmakd` left behind by an earlier run made **23 tests fail**, each reporting only its own "server did not become ready" timeout with the real reason (`bind: address already in use`) buried in a per-test `server.log` — it reads exactly like a code regression, and was mistaken for one before this fix. stmakd already exited 1 on the bind failure (that path was added deliberately after the same bug), but it bound **last**, inside the serve goroutine at the end of startup — so the conflict surfaced only after realtime was running, motmod was loaded, HAL threads were started and the interpreter was initialised: a fully started machine, torn straight back down, on hardware whose drives that startup may have energised. `createAPIServer` now opens the listener where it already runs (**before realtime**) and `startAPIServer` serves it; the same conflict is two log lines and exit 1 instead of ~60 lines of startup and a full teardown. Consequences handled: `stopAPIServer` closes a listener that was never served (`http.Server.Shutdown` only knows about listeners it is *serving*, so a startup that failed a later step would leak the socket — and the next start would hit "address already in use" caused by its own predecessor); between bind and serve the socket listens unaccepted, so an early client queues in the backlog rather than being refused; the **bound** address is logged, which is what a `:0` ephemeral configuration needs. `TestStopAPIServer_IdempotentAndConcurrent` moved to an ephemeral port — the bind is real now, and a unit test must not fail because the machine happens to be running LinuxCNC. **runtests** gained the matching up-front gate in the idiom of the existing `lsof` check: if the REST address is already listening, name the port, the holding pid and the remedy, and exit 2 before the first test (honours `STMAK_REST_ADDR`, reuses the `lsof` that gate already guarantees). A preflight cannot see a server leaked by an *earlier test*, so `tests/stmak-scale.sh` (sourced by every driver) gained `stmak_bind_failure`, which reads the reason out of the log the server already wrote; wired into `stmak_wait_ready`'s two failure paths and filestream-driver's. Tests: two launcher regressions (the bind error is reported and installs no listener; a never-served listener is released — asserted by re-binding the address, not by trusting `Close`). runtests 241/241. |
| 2026-07-22 | **G-1/G-2 fixed — the storage APIs can report a failure (`@rc_error`).** A GMI call that returned its payload by value had nowhere to put an error, so `--client-cgo` ended in a literal `return result, nil` and, symmetrically, the Go-provider trampoline collapsed `err != nil` into a zeroed struct: a sqlite failure and a missing row were the same event to every consumer. **New IDL annotation `@rc_error`** (documented in `src/gmi/idl/README.md`) declares the shape the fix needs — the `i32` return is the status channel, an `out` parameter carries the payload — and is deliberately opt-in, because a plain `i32` return next to an `out` param is a *value* the provider supplies itself (canon's `get_tool_by_number` returns `-1` for "not found" that way, and had to keep doing so). **G-2 (the emitter blocker) fixed:** `cgen/dispatch_c.go` had been unmarshalling an `out` param *from the request* and discarding what the provider wrote; it now declares the C variable, passes a pointer, checks the rc, and marshals the filled-in payload. **Slice payloads needed a new capability** — a callee-allocated slice cannot be a caller-provided buffer, so `[]T out` now travels in an owning `<api>_<elem>_slice_t {data, len}` (provider mallocs, caller frees, the same rule as a slice return), which is what let `get_entries`/`get_namespaces`/`list_tools` convert too rather than staying blind. **`persist.gmi` + `tooltable.gmi` fully converted (all 12 methods).** **Both Go signatures are unchanged on purpose** — consumer *and* provider stay `GetEntry(h, k) (Entry, error)` — so no Go call site moved; the churn is in C, where the payload became a pointer argument. **The REST/WS wire format is unchanged too**, and that is now proven mechanically: the emitters marshal a `restView(fn)` (payload out-param → return, out-param dropped from the request), and the regenerated `tooltable_client.ts` is **byte-identical** to the pre-conversion one. That check found a real break in passing: without the view, the TS client emitted `listTools(tools: ToolEntry[]): Promise<number>` — the status as the result and the payload as a query parameter. **C consumers updated — five, not the two the finding listed:** `cnc/iotask/ioControl_v2.c` *and* `ioControl.c` (both tool-change paths; new `tt_get_tool`/`tt_put_tool` wrappers, and the four previously *unchecked* `put_tool` calls in `load_tool` are now checked), `internal/task/interp_param_io_persist.c` (a failed restore is no longer an all-zero parameter set — it becomes "Unable to restore parameters"), `internal/ngcpreview/module.go` and `internal/launcher/retain.go` (a failed read no longer reads as "nothing stored", which would have overwritten the retained values on the next save). The last three were found by the compiler, not by the review. **Consequential semantic decision:** persist's `GetEntry` now returns the zero entry with a nil error for a missing row instead of an error — the status channel carries one bit, and folding "no such key" into it would make an unwritten key indistinguishable from a broken database (the exact confusion T-4 was about). "Absent" stays distinguishable from "present and empty": a stored row echoes its `Key` back. halscope's first start and tooltable's unstored tool already relied on that convention. **Fail-loud guards:** the checker rejects `@rc_error` without an `i32` return or an out param, together with `@returns_value`, with more than one out param on a REST route, a slice out param on a non-`@rc_error` func, and more than one slice out param. **Tests:** generator tests for the dispatch/bridge/client emission and the REST-view invariants (**2 mutation-verified**), six new checker rejection cases, an end-to-end `tooltable` test that breaks the backing store and asserts each method reports it *with the rc* (proving the failure crossed the C callback boundary), and a `persist` REST-dispatch test pinning the response bodies and that an invalid handle is an error rather than an empty 200. Full build, `go vet`, gofmt clean; `-race` green; full runtests round owed by the ruling — run. Out of scope by the ruling and unchanged: `emcio.GetStatus` and the `@returns_value` contracts. |

Fire-and-forget is *kept* — waiting on a QoS≥1 token would stall `publishLoop` — but the token is now peeked (`select` on `Done()` with a `default`), and what counts as failure is what is knowable without blocking: a disconnected client, or a token already completed with an error. On failure the count does not advance, a new `publish-error-count` pin does, the log is throttled to one line per streak, and the change shadow is **not** updated so the next tick retries the value instead of swallowing it. **N9 (apiserver):** maintainer ruling — cap both axes, INI-configurable. `REST_MAX_CONNECTIONS` (256) and `REST_MAX_WS_CONNECTIONS` (64); two caps because a WebSocket is a hijacked HTTP connection holding an accept slot for its whole life, so one cap lets watch clients starve plain REST. Phase-5 rows apiserver/halrest/mqttbridge/halscope → `U`/`FP` ✅. `internal/inirest` (57.5 %) is the one Phase-5 module the pass did not cover. |
