| 2026-07-21 | **gmicompile: nullable scalar params now carried as pointers through the C ABI** (commit `4e1f2ac387`; the root fix for the issue-#265 class, maintainer ruling: C-ABI pointers over the Go-native-dispatch alternative). A nullable `T?` scalar param was flattened to a plain C scalar in the callback FFI, so "absent" was lost — the dispatch zero-filled it and the bridge trampoline always took `&local`, so a Go provider always saw a non-nil pointer to a fabricated 0 (`newthread cpu`→0/rejected, `newthread fp`→false/non-FP, `addf position`→0/front-insert). Now transits as `const T *` (NULL=absent) across api.h typedef / `call_X` / dispatch marshaling (malloc+`_freeList`) / bridge trampoline (nil-preserving `*T`); strings excluded (already `char*`). The one C provider with such params (`src/hal/drivers/ethercat/gmi_ethercat.c` — `master_index` ×32, `sdo_upload size?`, `soe_read mem_size?`) updated to pointer signatures (`resolve_master` takes the pointer, NULL→master 0; callers unchanged); stmakd + ethercat.so rebuild **0 C warnings**. CLI band-aids (HC-4 -1 sentinels) reverted to nil — `tests/newthread-runtime` now exercises the omitted→nil→NULL→impl path end-to-end and passes. cgen regression tests (`nullable_param_test.go`). generated/gmi/* is gitignored (regenerated at build). Validated by a full runtests round (all green). Belongs to gmicompile hotspot #2. |
| 2026-07-21 | **GitHub issue #265 (`newthread` at runtime) FIXED** (commit `b4c7ffb74a`; Phase-4 halcmd, HC-4). `halcmd newthread <name> <period>` (no cpu) against a running server failed `cpu=0 is not an isolated CPU (isolated: [])` on a no-isolcpus box, while `newthread` in a HAL file worked. Root cause: the `.hal` parser defaults cpu to -1 (auto), but the CLI left it nil, and a nil `i32?` is flattened to 0 across the cgo REST dispatch (`int32_t` ABI has no "absent"; halcmd registers as C callbacks so the cgo path runs, not the nil-preserving Go bridge) → the impl gets `&0` → cpu 0 is non-isolated → rejected. Fix: CLI defaults cpu to -1 and sends it explicitly; explicit `cpu=0` still correctly rejected. Reproduced + verified live; regression runtest `tests/newthread-runtime` (resident server + runtime newthread) passes `scripts/runtests`. **Follow-up flagged:** the nullable-scalar-through-cgo flattening is a gmicompile codegen limitation affecting any `T?` scalar the cgo dispatch handles (e.g. `addf` optional `position` → 0/insert-at-front vs append) — the general fix belongs to gmicompile (hotspot #2), regenerating all packages. |

| 2026-07-21 | **ADS cluster reviewed (Phase 2, Tier 2 adversarial)** — `ADS_REVIEW_FINDINGS.md`. Net-new code, no 2.9 oracle; at review time the server bound `0.0.0.0:48898` with no protocol auth. Two independent refutation passes (remote-DoS, concurrency/lifecycle). **Main result: four remote-DoS defects in the as-reviewed code, all fixed this pass.** Until the bounds checks landed, a malformed request of ~28 bytes could panic or exhaust memory in the server process: A1 SumWrite `uint32` overflow → slice panic; A2 unbounded `make` from client sub-request count (≈137 GB → OOM); A3 unbounded process-image read `length` (≈4 GB, incl. notification `sendLoop` re-OOM every 10 ms); A4 no `recover()` in any goroutine. | 2026-07-22 | **CRITICAL: `thread_lock` leaked locked on cooperative task exit → hung controller on `delthread`; fixed.** Found by unskipping the HAL thread-lifecycle tests (see the previous entry). **Scope: every deployment without RT privileges** — `rtapi_initialize_app()` sets `do_thread_lock = 1` whenever `harden_rt()` fails, i.e. the `SCHED_OTHER` fallback path, which covers sim/dev setups and any machine whose RT limits are not configured. **Mechanism:** `task_wrapper()` (`uspace_rtapi_lib.c`) acquired `thread_lock` at task start and *never released it*, relying on `task_wait()` to drop it on seeing the cooperative-exit flag. `task_wait()` does that on its two `task_exit` checks — but the flag can be set in the window *after* it re-acquires the lock and checks it, and the task loop's own `while (!self->task_exit)` condition then ends the task **holding** the lock. The mutex is left locked with its owner gone; the next `newthread` blocks forever on the acquire in `task_wrapper`, so it never reaches its loop, never observes its own exit flag, and the `pthread_join` inside `hal_thread_delete` → `rtapi_task_delete` never returns. **Symptom: `delthread` (or any module unload that deletes a thread) hangs the process forever, once a thread has already been created and deleted.** Confirmed with a native backtrace: one thread in `__lll_lock_wait` on `thread_lock` at `task_wrapper`, another in `pthread_join` at `hal_lib.c:2145`. **Fix:** lock ownership is now explicit — `struct rtapi_task.holds_thread_lock` (`rtapi/rtapi_task.h`, additive) is set/cleared at every acquire/release, and `task_wrapper()` releases the lock after the task body iff the task still holds it, so it is dropped exactly once on whichever path the task exits. The RT (`SCHED_FIFO`) path is unaffected — `do_thread_lock` is 0 there. This is the sibling of the earlier `task_wait()` shutdown-deadlock fix (same mutex, different escape path); that one covered the task that exits *inside* `task_wait`, this one the task that exits *after* it returns. **Regression test:** `internal/halcmd.TestThreadCreateDeleteCycles` — create/delete cycles with the delete walked across the thread period so some land mid-sleep and some just as the task re-takes the lock; **mutation-verified, hangs 5/5 with the fix reverted**, passes `-race -count=3` with it. (Two incidental facts pinned by the same work: the cycle counter advances from thread *creation*, not from `start` — `start`/`stop` gate funct execution and the `Running` flag, which is what the launcher's unload synchronisation relies on; and a thread faster than the established base period is rejected with EINVAL.) Owned by `RT_HARDENING_CHECKLIST.md`, updated there. |

| 2026-07-23 | **AXIS file-load UX trio fixed on real hardware — nc_files whitelist, exit stall, operator-error channel as sole error surface** (commits `81b3261e93`/`d4c5504c18`/`a3775cb8f9`). Follows the 2026-07-22 path-resolution migration below. **(1) Install-tree `nc_files` whitelisted** (`pathres.ProgramDirs`): opening a stock demo (`nc_files/3dtest.ngc`) from a config that lives elsewhere was refused "outside the allowed directories" — `ProgramDirs` added `<EMC2_HOME>/share` but never the system NC-files dir, though the path was already baked into `config.EMC2NCFilesDir` (`@EMC2_NCFILES_DIR@`) and unused; now resolved + appended, so both entry points that build their allow-list through it (task `program_open`, ngcpreview `get_file`/`gen_preview`) accept it. **(2) ~5 s AXIS exit stall fixed** (`gmi/_watch.py`): `WatchClient.stop()` waited on `run_coroutine_threadsafe(_shutdown).result(timeout)`, but that `concurrent.futures.Future` is resolved by a callback **on** the event loop — and `_shutdown` closes the socket, which ends the recv loop, returns `_main`, and stops+closes the loop **before** the cross-thread result callback can run, so `fut.result()` blocked the full 2 s on every clean shutdown while `_shutdown` itself finished in ~1 ms; AXIS tears down two clients at exit (position logger + message list) → the ~5 s. Now the thread join is the authoritative barrier (fire `_shutdown`, don't await its future; `_shutdown` cancels `_main` so a client parked in reconnect-backoff also wakes), the one await outside the try/except (backoff sleep) is guarded against the cancel. Measured `stop()`: 2.0 s → ~1–3 ms across alive / server-gone / mid-backoff, `stop_logger` final call still delivered. **(3) Operator-error channel made authoritative** (maintainer ruling): a refused command reported the same failure two-to-three times — the detailed message-list widget (from the operator-error channel), a **degraded** `command refused (HTTP 409)` AXIS toast (the base `_post` had already consumed the one-shot HTTPError body), and a raw `gmi.Command: <code> <url>: {json}` stderr dump. The message-list / operator-error channel is now the **sole** user-facing surface (classic NML behavior); the REST status is control-flow only — `AxisCommand._post` swallows the HTTPError and returns -1 (callers still branch on it, e.g. `open_file_guts` skips the preview fetch on a refused `program_open`) but pops **no** toast; base `_post` logs one concise stderr line + stashes the parsed message on `e.gmi_error`. Since the client no longer renders HTTP errors, an audit of the whole command surface closed the operator-actionable refusals that would otherwise go silent, **honoring per-command silence** — jog and hot paths stay quiet: `requireOn` split into `requireOnQuiet` (jog) + emitting `requireOn` (one edit covers ~8 commands' not-on gap); `requireNotEstop`/`requireProgram`/`canPowerOnLocked` emit; `preflightAuto`/`preflightManualMode` surface the `canSwitchMode` reason (`canSwitchMode` itself left silent so MDI keeps its own wording, no double-emit); `LoadToolTable`/`ToolUnload` emit on IO failure; `ProgramOpen`'s path-resolver refusal now shows a short operator message with the full allowed-roots detail kept in the log + returned error. Design ruling recorded in agent memory. **Validation: real-machine file-load test good; task-package build + `go test` green; Python edits compile; teardown timing verified by probe. Full `scripts/runtests` GREEN** — one flaky `motion/g0` failure that passed on retry (pre-existing test-stability flake, not a deterministic regression from this change; further investigation out of scope for now). |
